]
.

Adding the estimates of
\Sigma_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
above, we get

|||(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{A}_{(0)}\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}})(t)|||^{2}_{s-1,\ast}\leq C(K)|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}+C(K)||\mathbf{V}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}||\mathbf{V}||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}\,.

(6.18)

For the rest of the term in (
6.3
), using (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), we obtain that

\begin{split}|||\partial_{2}(\dot{H}^{+}_{2}\partial_{1}\hat{\Phi}^{+})(t)|||^{2}_{s-1,\ast,t}&\leq||\dot{H}^{+}_{2}\partial_{1}\hat{\Phi}^{+}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+2,\ast,t}\Big)\\
&\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t).\end{split}

(6.19)

The second term in (
6.3
) can be controlled similarly.
Summarizing from (
6.14
)-(
6.19
), we conclude that (
6.12
) holds.
∎

The following lemma gives the estimate of the normal derivative of the entropy, which is treated differently from the other components of the vector
{\mathbf{V}}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

The estimate

|||S^{\pm}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t)

(6.20)

holds for problem (
4.31
) for all
t\leq T
, where
s
is a positive integer and
\mathcal{M}(t)
is defined in (
6.6
).

Proof.

The linearized equation for the entropy
S^{\pm}
is an evolution-like equation because the coefficient of the normal derivative of the entropy vanishes on the boundary; this yields that no boundary conditions are needed to be coupled to the equation in order to derive an apriori energy estimate. So, to estimate
S^{\pm}
, we just handle the equation of
S^{\pm}
alone by the standard energy method tools.
The details of the proof are similar to those of the following Lemma
6.4
.
∎

Now, we derive weighted derivative estimates.

6.2.
Estimate of weighted derivatives

Since the differential operators
(\sigma\partial_{1})^{\alpha_{1}}
and
\sigma^{\alpha_{1}}\partial^{\alpha_{1}}_{1}
are equivalent, see
[
28
]
, in the following we discuss the term
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}
, with
\alpha_{1}>0
and
\langle\alpha\rangle=|\alpha|+\alpha_{3}\leq s
, in its equivalent form
\sigma^{\alpha_{1}}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}\mathbf{V}
, where
D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}:=\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{1}}_{1}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}
(
\alpha^{\prime}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
).

Lemma 6.4
.

The following estimate holds for (
4.31
) for all
t\leq T:

\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle\leq s,\alpha_{1}>0}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t),

(6.21)

where
s
is a positive integer and
\mathcal{M}(t)
is defined in (
6.6
).

Proof.

It is obvious that when
\alpha_{1}>0,

D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}|_{x_{1}=0}=0.
Note that

\sigma^{\alpha_{1}}\partial^{m+1}_{1}=\partial_{1}(\sigma^{\alpha_{1}}\partial^{m}_{1})-\alpha_{1}\sigma^{\prime}\sigma^{\alpha_{1}-1}\partial^{m}_{1},

for some nonnegative integer
m.

Applying
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}
to (
5.3
) and using the standard energy method,
we obtain that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{+}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})(t)d\mathbf{x}=\int_{\Omega_{t}}\Big(((\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{0}+\partial_{1}\mathcal{A}_{1}+\partial_{2}\mathcal{A}_{2})D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}+2\mathcal{R})\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\Big)d\mathbf{x}d\tau,

where

\quad\mathcal{R}=D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{R}_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{1},\quad\mathcal{R}_{0}=\alpha_{1}\sigma^{\prime}\mathcal{A}_{1}\sigma^{\alpha_{1}-1}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}},

\mathcal{R}_{1}=-[D^{\alpha}_{\ast},\mathcal{A}_{0}]\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}-\sum^{2}_{j=1}[D^{\alpha}_{\ast},\mathcal{A}_{j}]\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}-D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\mathcal{A}_{3}{\mathbf{V}})

(the brackets
[\cdot,\cdot]
here denotes the commutator between the operators).
Notice that
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is positive definite, then

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{+}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})(t)d\mathbf{x}\geq c_{0}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\mathbf{V}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})},

where
c_{0}
depends on the number
k
in (
4.2
) and (
4.4
). Hence, we obtain that

||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{+})}\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\mathcal{R}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\Big).

(6.22)

Now, we estimate
||\mathcal{R}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}
in (
6.22
). Recall that
\mathcal{R}=D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{R}_{0}+\mathcal{R}_{1}.

Using Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), we can prove that

\begin{split}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\mathcal{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}&\lesssim||J^{T}\mathbf{F}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||\mathbf{F}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\mathbf{F}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.23)

Using the decomposition of boundary matrix
\mathcal{A}_{1}
in (
5.5
),
\mathcal{A}_{1}=\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{A}_{(0)}
, following arguments similar to those used in Lemma
6.1
, we obtain that

\begin{split}||\mathcal{R}_{0}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}\sigma^{\alpha_{1}-1}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}+||\frac{\mathcal{A}_{(0)}}{\sigma}\sigma^{\alpha_{1}}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\\
&\lesssim C(K)\left(||\sigma^{\alpha_{1}-1}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{n}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}+||\sigma^{\alpha_{1}}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{t,x}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\right)\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{n}||^{2}_{s-1,\ast,t}+||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big)\\
&\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t),\end{split}

(6.24)

recall here above that the matrix
\mathcal{A}
acts only on the noncharacteristic part
{\mathbf{V}}_{n}
of the unknown
\mathbf{V}
.

Now, we estimate the commutators in
\mathcal{R}_{1}:

For
j=0,2,

we obtain that

\begin{split}||[D^{\alpha}_{\ast},\mathcal{A}_{j}]\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\leq&\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\,.\end{split}

(6.25)

For
\langle\beta\rangle=1
, we get

\sum_{\langle\beta\rangle=1\,,\,\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C(K)||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\,.

(6.26)

For
\langle\beta\rangle\geq 2,
we obtain that

\begin{split}\sum_{\langle\beta\rangle\geq 2\,,\,\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}&\lesssim\sum_{\langle\beta^{\prime}\rangle=2,\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}(D^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j})D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}(\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s-1,\ast,t}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big)\,.\end{split}

(6.27)

For
j=1,
we need to be very careful. We have

\begin{split}||[D^{\alpha}_{\ast},\mathcal{A}_{1}]\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}&\lesssim\sum_{0<\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{1}D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}(\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\,.\end{split}

(6.28)

For
\langle\beta\rangle=1
we get
\beta_{3}=0.
Therefore, from (
5.5
) it follows that
D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{1}|_{x_{1}=0}=0
and we use Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
), (
3.8
) to obtain

\sum_{\langle\beta\rangle=1\,,\,\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{1}D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}(\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C(K)||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\,.

(6.29)

For
\langle\beta\rangle\geq 2
, we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{\langle\beta\rangle\geq 2\,,\,\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{1}D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}(\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\langle\beta^{\prime}\rangle=2,\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta\leq\alpha}||D^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}(D^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{1})D^{\alpha-\beta}_{\ast}(\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.30)

Using Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
), (
3.8
), we can prove that

\begin{split}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\mathcal{A}_{3}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}&\lesssim||\mathcal{A}_{3}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+2,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.31)

Notice that summing up (
6.25
)-(
6.31
) gives

||\mathcal{R}_{1}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t).

(6.32)

Hence, using (
6.23
), (
6.24
) and (
6.32
), we obtain (
6.21
). Lemma
6.4
is concluded.
∎

Now, we are going to estimate the non-weighted normal derivatives.

6.3.
Estimate of non-weighted normal derivatives

Now we perform the differential operator
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}
, in the case
\alpha_{1}=0,\alpha_{3}\geq 1
, that is
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}=\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}
with
\langle\alpha\rangle\leq s
. Now, we are ready to prove the following Lemma
6.5
.

Lemma 6.5
.

The estimate

\sum_{\langle\alpha\rangle\leq s,\alpha_{1}=0,\alpha_{3}\geq 1}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t)

(6.33)

holds for problem (
4.31
) for all
t\leq T,
where
s
is a positive integer,
\mathcal{M}(t)
is given in (
6.6
).

Proof.

Similar as in Lemma
6.4
, applying the operator
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}
on (
5.3
) and using the standard energy method,
we obtain that

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{+}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})(t)d\mathbf{x}&=\int_{\Omega_{t}}\Big(((\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{0}+\partial_{1}\mathcal{A}_{1}+\partial_{2}\mathcal{A}_{2})D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}+2\mathcal{R})\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\Big)d\mathbf{x}d\tau\\
&\quad+\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(\mathcal{A}_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\,,\end{split}

where
\mathcal{R}
is defined as in the proof of Lemma
6.4
. Thus

||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\mathcal{R}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}+\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(t)\Big),

where

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(\mathcal{A}_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\Big|=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{u}_{n})|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\Big|.

When
\alpha_{1}=0,
by definition,
\mathcal{R}_{0}=0.

Hence, we obtain the estimate

||\mathcal{R}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t).

This yields that

||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{+})}\leq C(K)(\mathcal{M}(t)+\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(t)).

(6.34)

Using (
6.3
) and (
6.4
), we obtain that

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(t)\lesssim||\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\mathcal{K}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}\,,

where
\mathcal{K}
is defined in (
6.4
). Moreover, recalling that
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}|_{x_{1}=0}=\mathrm{diag}\Big(\mathcal{E}_{12},-\mathcal{E}_{12}\Big)
because
\partial_{1}\hat{\Phi}^{\pm}|_{x_{1}=0}=\pm 1,
we notice that
||\tilde{\mathcal{A}}|_{x_{1}=0}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{t})}=1.
Using
\mathcal{A}_{(0)}|_{x_{1}=0}=0

we obtain that

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}(t)\leq C\Big(\Sigma_{1}(t)+\Sigma_{2}(t)+\Sigma_{3}(t)+||\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{3}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\mathcal{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}\Big),

(6.35)

where

\Sigma_{1}(t)=\sum_{j=0,2}||\mathcal{A}_{j}\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})},

\Sigma_{2}(t)=\sum_{j=0,2}\sum_{\langle\beta^{\prime}\rangle\geq 1,\beta^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime\prime}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}-1)}||D^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\beta^{\prime\prime}}_{\ast}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})};

notice that in spite of the notation here
D^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}
and
D^{\beta^{\prime\prime}}_{\ast}
dot not involve any weighted derivative
\sigma\partial_{1}
(see the beginning of this section); notice also that
\beta^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime\prime}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}-1)
implies that
\langle\beta^{\prime}\rangle+\langle\beta^{\prime\prime}\rangle\leq s-2
;

\Sigma_{3}(t)=\sum_{\alpha_{3}^{\prime}+\alpha_{3}^{\prime\prime}\leq\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3}^{\prime},\alpha_{3}^{\prime\prime}\geq 1}||\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}(\partial^{\alpha_{3}^{\prime}}_{1}\mathcal{A}_{(0)}\partial^{\alpha_{3}^{\prime\prime}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}.

Now, we estimate
\Sigma_{2}(t)
and
\Sigma_{3}(t)
, which can be controlled by using Lemma
3.4
(i) and Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
):

\begin{split}\Sigma_{2}(t)&\lesssim\sum_{j=0,2}\sum_{\langle\beta^{\prime}\rangle=1}\Big(||D^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s-3,\ast,t}+||\partial_{1}(D^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\ast}\mathcal{A}_{j}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{s-3,\ast,t}\Big)\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.36)

In the above estimate, it is noted that
\langle\beta^{\prime\prime}\rangle\leq s-3
.

\begin{split}\Sigma_{3}(t)&\lesssim||\partial_{1}\mathcal{A}_{(0)}\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s-4,\ast,t}+||\partial_{1}(\partial_{1}\mathcal{A}_{(0)}\partial_{1}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{s-4,\ast,t}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.37)

For the term
\Sigma_{1}(t),
passing to the volume integral then using Leibniz’s rule, we get (for shortness, in the sequel we denote
D^{\alpha}:=\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}
whereas
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}:=\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{1}
):

\begin{split}\Sigma_{1}(t)&=-\sum_{j=0,2}\int_{\Omega_{t}}\partial_{1}|\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}|^{2}d\mathbf{x}d\tau\\
&=-2\sum_{j=0,2}\int_{\Omega_{t}}\Big((\mathcal{A}^{2}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}})\\
&\quad+(\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\partial_{1}\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}})\Big)d\mathbf{x}d\tau\,.\end{split}

Then integration by parts with respect to
\partial_{j}
(for
j=0,2)
gives

\Sigma_{1}(t)=\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}(t)+J_{0}(t)\,,

where

\begin{split}\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}(t)&=2\sum_{j=0,2}\int_{\Omega_{t}}\Big(\mathcal{A}^{2}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial^{2}_{j}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\Big)\\
&\quad+\Big((\mathcal{A}_{j}\partial_{j}\mathcal{A}_{j}+\partial_{j}\mathcal{A}_{j}\mathcal{A}_{j})D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\Big)\\
&\quad-\Big(\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\partial_{1}\mathcal{A}_{j}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{j}{\mathbf{V}}\Big)d\mathbf{x}d\tau,\end{split}

J_{0}(t)=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\Big(\mathcal{A}^{2}_{0}D^{\alpha}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\Big)(t)d\mathbf{x}.

Since
|\alpha|+2+2(\alpha_{3}-1)\leq s
we obtain that

\tilde{\Sigma}_{1}(t)\leq C(K)||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}.

(6.38)

Using Young’s inequality, we obtain that

J_{0}(t)\leq C(K)\Big(\varepsilon||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)||||^{2}_{s-1,\ast}\Big),

(6.39)

for small
\varepsilon.

Therefore, we conclude from (
6.38
), (
6.39
) and elementary inequalities (
6.13
) that

\Sigma_{1}(t)\leq C(K)\Big(\varepsilon||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}\Big).

(6.40)

The last two terms in (
6.35
) can be estimated by using Lemma
3.4
(i), Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
):

||\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{3}{\mathbf{V}})||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}\partial^{\alpha_{3}-1}_{1}\mathcal{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t).

(6.41)

Summarizing (
6.34
)-(
6.37
) and using (
6.40
) and (
6.41
), taking
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, we get the estimate (
6.33
). Therefore, Lemma
6.5
is proved.
∎

6.4.
Estimate of non-weighted tangential derivatives

Now, we are going to obtain estimates of non-weighted tangential derivatives, i.e.
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3}=0
, that is
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}=\partial^{\alpha_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{2}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
. This is the most important case because we shall use the boundary conditions. This gives the loss of two additional derivatives that imply that in final tame estimate we will have the “
s+4,\ast,t
” loss of derivatives from the coefficients, see Theorem
6.1
. This loss is caused by the presence of zero order terms in
\varphi
(see (
4.16
)).

Lemma 6.6
.

The following estimate holds for (
4.31
) for all
t\leq T:

\begin{split}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s\,,\,\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3}=0}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}&{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}\leq\varepsilon C(K)\Big(|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}+|||\varphi(t)|||^{2}_{H^{s-1}({\mathbb{R}})}\Big)\\
&+\frac{C(K)}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathcal{M}(t)+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Gamma_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+4,\ast,t}\Big),\end{split}

(6.42)

where
s
is positive integer,
\varepsilon
is a positive constant and
\mathcal{M}(t)
is defined in (
6.6
).

Proof.

We only need to estimate the highest-order tangential derivatives with
|\alpha|=s,
since the lower order terms can be controlled by definition of the anisotropic Sobolev norm, through the following estimate:

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s-1}||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}\leq C||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}.

(6.43)

Therefore,
applying same argument as in Lemmata
6.4
and
6.5
, we obtain that

||D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}\leq C(K)(\mathcal{M}(t)+\mathcal{J}(t)),

(6.44)

for
\alpha=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{2}),
with
|\alpha|=s,
where

\mathcal{J}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(\mathcal{B}_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\Big|.

Taking into account the boundary conditions (
4.31
) and (
4.33
), and also the important dissipative structure (
5.8
), the explicit quadratic boundary term in the integral can be rewritten in a suitable form: let us denote

c^{\pm}=\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|=s,|\alpha^{\prime}|\geq 1}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\ast}\hat{\lambda}^{\pm}D^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\ast}\dot{H}^{\pm}_{n},\quad\dot{w}^{\pm}=\dot{u}^{\pm}_{n}-\hat{\lambda}^{\pm}\dot{H}^{\pm}_{n}\,,

where

[\partial_{1}\hat{q}]=(\partial_{1}\hat{q}^{+}+\partial_{1}\hat{q}^{-})|_{x_{1}=0}\,,\,\,\dot{w}^{\pm}|_{x_{1}=0}=(\dot{u}^{\pm}_{N}-\hat{\lambda}^{\pm}\dot{H}^{\pm}_{N})|_{x_{1}=0}

and
\hat{\lambda}^{\pm}
were defined in (
5.10
), see also Lemma
5.1
.

The boundary quadratic form becomes:

\begin{split}(\mathcal{B}_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot D^{\alpha}_{\ast}{\mathbf{V}})|_{x_{1}=0}&=2[(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{u}_{N}-\hat{\lambda}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{H}_{N})D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}]=2[cD^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}]+2[D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{w}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}]\\
&=2[cD^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}]+2D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{w}^{-}|_{x_{1}=0}[D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}]+2D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}|_{x_{1}=0}[D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{w}]\\
&=2D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}|_{x_{1}=0}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\partial_{2}\varphi[\hat{u}_{2}-\hat{\lambda}\hat{H}_{2}])+\text{l.o.t},\end{split}

(6.45)

where lower order term l.o.t can be expressed by

2[cD^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}]-2D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{w}^{-}|_{x_{1}=0}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}([\partial_{1}\hat{q}]\varphi)-2D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}|_{x_{1}=0}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}([\partial_{1}\hat{u}_{N}-\hat{\lambda}\partial_{1}\hat{H}_{N}]\varphi).

Since the boundary conditions are dissipative, it is noted that first term on the right-hand side of (
6.45
) vanish by Lemma
5.1
.
The boundary terms can be estimated separately:

\mathcal{J}(t)\leq\sum_{\pm}\sum^{4}_{i=1}\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{i}(t),

(6.46)

where

\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{1}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(c^{\pm}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{\pm})|_{x_{1}=0}d{x_{2}}d\tau\Big|,

\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{2}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{w}^{-}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\varphi\partial_{1}\hat{q}^{\pm}))|_{x_{1}=0}d{x_{2}}d\tau\Big|,

\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{3}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\varphi\partial_{1}\hat{u}^{\pm}_{n}))|_{x_{1}=0}d{x_{2}}d\tau\Big|,

\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{4}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\varphi\hat{\lambda}^{\pm}\partial_{1}\hat{H}^{\pm}_{n}))|_{x_{1}=0}d{x_{2}}d\tau\Big|.

Recall that
|\alpha|=s\geq 2.
We denote
D^{\alpha}_{\ast}=\partial_{l}D^{\gamma}
,
D^{\gamma}:=\partial^{\gamma_{0}}_{t}\partial^{\gamma_{2}}_{2}
for
\gamma=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{2}),|\gamma|=s-1\geq 1
, where

l=\begin{cases}2,&\text{ if }\alpha_{0}\neq s,\\
0,&\text{ if }\alpha_{0}=s.\end{cases}

In the following estimate of
\mathcal{J}^{+}_{1}(t)
, we separate the analysis into two cases.

Case A:
When
\alpha_{0}=s
, then
l=0,D^{\alpha}_{\ast}=\partial_{t}D^{\gamma}.
Using the normal derivative estimates (
6.5
), (
6.12
) for non-characteristic variables, the elementary inequality (
6.13
) and integration by parts, we obtain that

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}^{+}_{1}(t)&=\Big|\int_{\Omega_{t}}(\partial_{t}c^{+}\partial_{1}D^{\gamma}\dot{q}^{+}-\partial_{1}c^{+}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+})d\mathbf{x}d\tau-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}c^{+}\partial_{1}D^{\gamma}\dot{q}^{+}d\mathbf{x}\Big|\\
&\leq C\Big(||\dot{q}^{+}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\partial_{1}\dot{q}^{+}||^{2}_{s-1,\ast,t}+||\partial_{t}c^{+}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\\
&\quad+||\partial_{1}c^{+}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}||c^{+}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}+\varepsilon||\partial_{1}\dot{q}^{+}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}\Big)\\
&\leq C\Big(||\dot{q}^{+}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\partial_{1}\dot{q}^{+}||^{2}_{s-1,\ast,t}+||\partial_{t}c^{+}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}\\
&\quad+||\partial_{1}c^{+}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega_{t})}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}||c^{+}(t)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}+\varepsilon|||\partial_{1}\dot{q}^{+}(t)|||^{2}_{s-1,\ast}\Big)\\
&\leq\varepsilon C(K)|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}+\frac{C(K)}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}(t).\end{split}

(6.47)

Here,
\varepsilon
is an arbitrary fixed constant.
Similar argument also holds for
\mathcal{J}^{-}_{1}(t).

Therefore, we obtain that

\mathcal{J}^{+}_{1}(t)+\mathcal{J}^{-}_{1}(t)\leq\varepsilon C(K)|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}+\frac{C(K)}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{M}(t).

Case B:
When
\alpha_{0}\neq s
, then
l=2,D^{\alpha}_{\ast}=\partial_{2}D^{\gamma}.
Using integration by parts, we could deduce that

|\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{1}(t)|\leq C(K)\mathcal{M}(t).

(6.48)

Next, we estimate
\mathcal{J}^{+}_{3}
and separate this term into two parts

\mathcal{J}^{+}_{3}\leq\mathcal{J}_{0}(t)+\Sigma_{4}(t),

where

\mathcal{J}_{0}(t)=\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(\varphi D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\partial_{1}\hat{u}^{+}_{n})|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\Big|,

\Sigma_{4}(t)=\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|=s,|\alpha^{\prime}|\geq 1}\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\ast}\varphi D^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\ast}\partial_{1}\hat{u}^{+}_{n})\Big|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\Big|.

For the term
\mathcal{J}_{0}(t),
after integrating by parts, we obtain that

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{0}(t)&=\Big|\int_{\Omega_{t}}(\partial_{1}\partial_{l}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\hat{u}^{+}_{n})\varphi\partial_{1}D^{\gamma}\dot{q}^{+}-(\partial^{2}_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\hat{u}^{+}_{n})\varphi D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}\\
&\quad+\partial_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\hat{u}^{+}_{n}\partial_{l}\varphi\partial_{1}D^{\gamma}\dot{q}^{+}d\mathbf{x}d\tau-\frac{l-2}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}(\partial_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\hat{u}^{+}_{n})\varphi\partial_{1}D^{\gamma}\dot{q}^{+}d\mathbf{x}\Big|.\end{split}

We note that to estimate
\partial^{2}_{1}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\hat{u}^{+}_{n}
, we need
\hat{W}
with regularity
s+4.

For the term
\mathcal{J}_{0}(t),
using Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
), (
3.8
), (
6.5
) and (
6.12
) we get

\mathcal{J}_{0}(t)\leq\varepsilon C(K)|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}+\frac{C(K)}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathcal{M}(t)+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}{(\Gamma_{t})}}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+4,\ast,t}\Big).

Now, we start to estimate
\Sigma_{4}.
It is noted that for
|\alpha^{\prime}|\geq 1,
we can isolate one tangential derivative in the differential operator:

D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\ast}\varphi=D^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\partial_{j}\varphi),\quad|\gamma^{\prime}|\leq s-1,\quad j=0\text{ or }j=2.

Using (
B.55
) and (
B.42
), we obtain that

\nabla_{t,x_{2}}\varphi=\mathbf{H}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\varphi}){\mathbf{V}}_{n}|_{x_{1}=0}+\mathbf{G}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\varphi})\varphi.

(6.49)

Here
\mathbf{H}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\varphi}),\mathbf{G}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\varphi})
depend on
\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}|_{x_{1}=0},\partial_{1}\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}|_{x_{1}=0}
and second order derivatives of
\hat{\varphi}.

Using (
6.49
), we can write the derivatives of
\varphi
by using the non-characteristic unknown
{\mathbf{V}}_{n}
. We insert these derivatives
D^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\ast}\varphi=D^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\cdots)
, with
|\gamma^{\prime}|\leq s-1
, into
\Sigma_{4}
to obtain

\Sigma_{4}(t)=\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|=s,|\alpha^{\prime}|\geq 1}\Big|\int_{\Gamma_{t}}(D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\dot{q}^{+}D^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbf{H}{\mathbf{V}}_{n}+\mathbf{G}\varphi)D^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\ast}\partial_{1}\hat{u}^{+}_{n})\Big|_{x_{1}=0}dx_{2}d\tau\Big|\,,

that can be controlled by the right-hand side of (
6.42
). Indeed, by passing to the volume integral on
\Omega_{t}
, we note that the highest order of regularity for
D^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\ast}\partial_{1}^{2}\hat{u}^{+}_{n}
is
s+3
because
|\gamma^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|=s-1
(since
|\alpha^{\prime}|=|\gamma^{\prime}|+1
and
|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|=s
). Similar estimates also hold for
\mathcal{J}^{-}_{3},\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{2},\mathcal{J}^{\pm}_{4}.
Using (
6.43
), (
6.44
), (
6.46
), (
6.47
), (
6.48
), we obtain the estimate (
6.42
). Therefore, Lemma
6.6
is concluded.
∎

6.5.
Estimate of front

Now, we are going to estimate the front
\varphi(t)
in
H^{s-1}({\mathbb{R}})
and its tangential derivatives
\nabla_{t,x_{2}}\varphi
in
H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t}).

Lemma 6.7
.

Given the solution
\varphi
of (
4.31
), for all
t\leq T
and positive integer
s,
the following estimate holds:

\begin{split}&|||\varphi(t)|||^{2}_{H^{s-1}({\mathbb{R}})}+||\nabla_{t,x_{2}}\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}\\
&\quad\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+(||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Gamma_{t})})||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+2,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.50)

Proof.

Applying tangential derivatives on the first boundary conditions in (
4.31
), we obtain that

\begin{split}|||\varphi(t)|||^{2}_{H^{s-1}({\mathbb{R}})}&\leq C(K)\Big(||\dot{u}_{n}|_{x_{1}=0}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq s-1}||\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}\Big),\end{split}

(6.51)

where

\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}=D^{\alpha}_{\ast}(\varphi\partial_{1}\hat{u}^{+}_{N})-([D^{\alpha}_{\ast},\hat{u}^{+}_{2}]\partial_{2}\varphi+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{2}\hat{u}^{+}_{2}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\varphi).

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
6.51
), using trace Theorem in Lemma
3.4
, we obtain that

||\dot{u}_{n}|_{x_{1}=0}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}\lesssim||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}.

(6.52)

For the third term on the right-hand side of (
6.51
), when
|\alpha|\leq s-1,
we obtain that

\begin{split}||\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}&\lesssim||\varphi\partial_{1}\hat{u}^{+}_{N}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+||[D^{\alpha}_{\ast},\hat{u}^{+}_{2}]\partial_{2}\varphi||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\partial_{2}\hat{u}^{+}_{2}D^{\alpha}_{\ast}\varphi||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{t})}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma_{t})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+2,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

(6.53)

Hence, we obtain the estimate of the first term in the left-hand side of (
6.50
).

For the estimate of the second term in the left-hand side of (
6.50
), we use the relation (
6.49
),
||\hat{\varphi}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{t})}\lesssim||\hat{\Psi}||_{s,\ast,t}\leq||\hat{\varphi}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{t})},
and Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) to obtain that

\begin{split}&||\nabla_{t,x_{2}}\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}\leq||\mathbf{H}(\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\varphi}){\mathbf{V}}_{n}|_{x_{1}=0}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+||\mathbf{G}(\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\varphi})\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,t}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{t})}+(||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{t})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma_{t})})||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+2,\ast,t}\Big).\end{split}

Therefore, Lemma
6.7
is concluded.
∎

Collecting all the previously established higher order estimates, we can prove the following lemma.

Lemma 6.8
.

The solution of the homogeneous problem (
4.31
) satisfies the following a priori estimate

||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C(K)Te^{C(K)T}\mathcal{N}(T)

(6.54)

for positive integer
s,
where

\begin{split}\mathcal{N}(T)&=||\mathbf{F}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+(||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{2,\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}+||\mathbf{F}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})})||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+4,\ast,T}.\end{split}

Proof.

Combining the estimates (
6.21
), (
6.33
), (
6.42
) choosing
\varepsilon
small enough and (
6.50
), we obtain that

\mathcal{I}(t)\leq C(K)\Big(\mathcal{N}(T)+\int^{t}_{0}\mathcal{I}(\tau)d\tau\Big),

(6.55)

where

\mathcal{I}(t)=|||{\mathbf{V}}(t)|||^{2}_{s,\ast}+|||\varphi(t)|||^{2}_{H^{s-1}({\mathbb{R}})}.

Notice that

\mathcal{I}(0)=0.

Then, using Grönwall’s lemma to (
6.55
), we obtain

\mathcal{I}(t)\leq C(K)e^{C(K)T}\mathcal{N}(T),\text{ for }0\leq t\leq T.

Integrating (
6.55
) with respect to
t\in(-\infty,T],
we get

||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C(K)Te^{C(K)T}\mathcal{N}(T).

(6.56)

Notice that
\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}=J{\mathbf{V}}.
Then, using the decomposition
J=J(\hat{W})=I+J_{0}(\hat{W}),
where
J_{0}
satisfies
J_{0}(0)=0,

we apply the Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) and derive

\begin{split}||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}&=||{\mathbf{V}}+J_{0}{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}\\
&\leq C(K)\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}\Big)\\
&\leq C(K)||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+TC(K)||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+1,\ast,T},\end{split}

(6.57)

and, using (
6.50
) with
t=T
,

\begin{split}&||\nabla_{t,x_{2}}\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\quad\lesssim\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}+(||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma_{T})})||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+2,\ast,T}\Big)\\
&\quad\lesssim\Big(||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\qquad+TC(K)(||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}+||\varphi||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma_{T})})||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+3,\ast,T}\Big)\,.\end{split}

(6.58)

In the proof of last inequalities in (
6.57
), (
6.58
), we have used the following relation by applying Sobolev imbedding:

\begin{split}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}&=\sum^{s}_{j=0}\int^{T}_{0}||\partial^{j}_{t}\hat{W}(t)||^{2}_{s-j,\ast}dt\leq\sum^{s}_{j=0}T\max_{t\in[0,T]}||\partial^{j}_{t}\hat{W}(t)||^{2}_{s-j,\ast}\\
&\lesssim T\sum^{s}_{j=0}\Big\{\int^{T}_{0}||\partial^{j}_{t}\hat{W}(t)||^{2}_{s-j,\ast}dt+\int^{T}_{0}||\partial^{j+1}_{t}\hat{W}(t)||^{2}_{s-j,\ast}dt\Big\}\\
&\lesssim T\int^{T}_{0}|||\partial^{j}_{t}\hat{W}(t)|||^{2}_{s+1-j,\ast}dt\lesssim T||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+1,\ast,T}.\end{split}

Using (
6.56
) and (
6.57
), we obtain

||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||^{2}_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C(K)Te^{C(K)T}\mathcal{N}(T).

(6.59)

Similar to (
6.57
), we can get

||{\mathbf{V}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}\leq C(K)||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{s,\ast,T}+TC(K)||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}||\hat{W}||^{2}_{s+1,\ast,T}.

(6.60)

Adding (
6.58
) and (
6.59
), and using (
6.60
), we conclude Lemma
6.8
.
∎

Using (
6.54
), we are ready to prove the tame estimate for the homogeneous problem (
4.31
).

Proof of Theorem
6.1
:  Using Lemma
6.8
, the Sobolev inequalities (
3.9
), (
3.10
) for
s\geq 6,
we obtain

\begin{split}||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}&\leq C(K)T^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{C(K)T}\Big(||\mathbf{F}||_{s,\ast,T}+(||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{6,\ast,T}\\
&\quad+||\varphi||_{H^{6}(\Gamma_{T})}+||\mathbf{F}||_{4,\ast,T})||\hat{W}||_{s+4,\ast,T}\Big).\end{split}

(6.61)

Taking
T
sufficiently small and
s=6,
using (
6.1
), we obtain that

||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{6,\ast,T}+||\varphi||_{H^{6}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C(K_{0})||\mathbf{F}||_{6,\ast,T}.

(6.62)

Hence, (
6.61
) and (
6.62
) implies (
6.2
). The existence and uniqueness of the solution comes from Theorem
5.1
. The proof of Theorem
6.1
is complete.

7.
Higher order energy estimate for problem (
4.20
)

Now, we are ready to obtain an a
priori
tame estimate in
H^{s}_{\ast}
for the nonhomogeneous problem (
4.20
).

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
T>0
and
s
be an integer,
s\geq 6.

Assume that the basic state
(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\varphi})
satisfies (
4.2
)-(
4.10
), and
(\breve{\mathbf{U}}^{\pm},\hat{\varphi})\in H^{s+4}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})\times H^{s+5}(\Gamma_{T})
satisfies (
6.1
).
Assume that
\mathbf{f}\in H^{s+2}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})
,
\mathbf{g}\in H^{s+2}(\Gamma_{T})
vanish in the past. Then, there exists a positive constant
K_{0},
that does not depend on
s
and
T,
and there exists a constant
C(K_{0})>0
such that if
\hat{K}\leq K_{0}
, then there exists a unique solution
(\dot{{\mathbf{U}}},\varphi)\in H^{s}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})\times H^{s}({\Gamma_{T}})
to the problem (
4.20
) that allows the tame estimate

\begin{split}&||\dot{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{s,\ast,T}+||\varphi||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\quad\leq C(K_{0})\Big(||\mathbf{f}||_{s+2,\ast,T}+||\mathbf{g}||_{H^{s+2}(\Gamma_{T})}+(||\mathbf{f}||_{8,\ast,T}+||\mathbf{g}||_{H^{8}(\Gamma_{T})})||\hat{W}||_{s+4,\ast,T}\Big),\end{split}

(7.1)

for
T
small enough, where
\hat{W}=(\breve{\mathbf{U}},\nabla_{t,\bf x}\hat{\Psi}).

Remark 7.1
.

The lower regularity in (
6.1
) and low norms in (
6.2
) and (
7.1
), for both the even and odd case, differ from Trakhinin
[
39
]
, see Theorem 3 and Theorem 4, due to finer Sobolev imbeddings (
3.9
), (
3.10
).

Proof.

Using the Moser-type calculus inequalities (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), we obtain a refined version of estimate (
4.27
) in tame form:

||\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{e}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\Psi})\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{s,\ast,T}\leq C(K)\Big(||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{s+2,\ast,T}+||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}||\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\Psi}||_{s+2,\ast,T}\Big).

Then, using Sobolev embedding inequalities (
3.10
), we get

||\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{e}(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\Psi})\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{s,\ast,T}\leq C(K)\Big(||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{s+2,\ast,T}+||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}||_{6,\ast,T}||\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\Psi}||_{s+2,\ast,T}\Big).

Using the above estimate, (
4.30
) and recalling the definition of
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}
, see Section
4.3
, it holds that

\begin{split}||\mathbf{F}||_{s,\ast,T}\leq C(K)\Big(||\mathbf{f}||_{s+2,\ast,T}+||\mathbf{g}||_{H^{s+2}(\Gamma_{T})}+(||\mathbf{f}||_{8,\ast,T}+||\mathbf{g}||_{H^{8}(\Gamma_{T})})||\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\Psi}||_{s+2,\ast,T}\Big).\end{split}

(7.2)

Using the assumption (
6.1
) and (
7.2
) with
s=6
, we get

||\mathbf{F}||_{6,\ast,T}\leq C(K_{0})\Big({||\mathbf{f}||_{8,\ast,T}+||\mathbf{g}||_{H^{8}(\Gamma_{T})}}\Big).

(7.3)

Combining the estimates (
6.61
), (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), we obtain the tame estimate (
7.1
).
∎

8.
Construction of Approximate Solutions

Suppose the initial data

({\mathbf{U}}^{\pm}_{0},\varphi_{0})=(\bar{\mathbf{U}}^{\pm}+\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\pm}_{0},\varphi_{0})=(p^{\pm}_{0},u^{\pm}_{1,0},u^{\pm}_{2,0},H^{\pm}_{1,0},H^{\pm}_{2,0},S^{\pm}_{0},\varphi_{0})

satisfy the stability condition (
4.4
) and restriction (
2.5
) at
x_{1}=0
for
x_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since
{H}^{+}_{2,0}\neq 0
or
{H}^{-}_{2,0}\neq 0
at
{x_{1}=0}
, see also Remark
4.3
, from (
2.5
) we can solve
\partial_{2}\varphi
as follows (we drop the sub-index 0 for simplicity):

\partial_{2}\varphi=\mu({\mathbf{U}})|_{x_{1}=0}=\frac{H^{+}_{1}H^{+}_{2}+H^{-}_{1}H^{-}_{2}}{(H^{+}_{2})^{2}+(H^{-}_{2})^{2}}\Big|_{x_{1}=0}\,,

(8.1)

where
{\mathbf{U}}:=({\mathbf{U}}^{+},{\mathbf{U}}^{-}).
Then, using the boundary condition (
4.3
), we have

\partial_{t}\varphi=\eta({\mathbf{U}})|_{x_{1}=0},

(8.2)

with

\eta({\mathbf{U}})=u^{+}_{1}-u^{+}_{2}\mu({\mathbf{U}}).

By using the hyperbolicity condition (
1.6
), we can write the system in (
2.1
):

\partial_{t}{\mathbf{U}}=-(A_{0}({\mathbf{U}}))^{-1}\Big(\tilde{A}_{1}({\mathbf{U}},\Psi)\partial_{1}{\mathbf{U}}+A_{2}({\mathbf{U}})\partial_{2}{\mathbf{U}}\Big),

(8.3)

where
\Psi:=(\Psi^{+},\Psi^{-})
, and the matrices
A_{0},A_{2},\tilde{A}_{1}
are defined by (
1.5
) and (
2.3
).
The traces

{\mathbf{U}}_{j}=(p^{+}_{j},u^{+}_{1,j},u^{+}_{2,j},H^{+}_{1,j},H^{+}_{2,j},S^{+}_{j},p^{-}_{j},u^{-}_{1,j},u^{-}_{2,j},H^{-}_{1,j},H^{-}_{2,j},S^{-}_{j})=\partial^{j}_{t}{\mathbf{U}}|_{t=0}

and

\varphi_{j}=\partial^{j}_{t}\varphi|_{t=0},\quad j\geq 1

can be defined step by step by applying operator
\partial^{j-1}_{t}
to (
8.2
) and (
8.3
), for
j\geq 1
and evaluating
\partial^{j}_{t}{\mathbf{U}}
and
\partial^{j}_{t}\varphi
at
t=0
in terms of the initial data. Notice that

\Psi^{\pm}_{j}=\partial^{j}_{t}\Psi^{\pm}|_{t=0}=\chi(\pm x_{1})\varphi_{j}.

Define the zero-th order compatibility condition:

[u_{1,0}]-[u_{2,0}]\partial_{2}\varphi_{0}=0,\quad[p_{0}+\frac{|H_{0}|^{2}}{2}]=0.

(8.4)

Taking (
8.1
), (
8.2
) evaluated at
t=0,
and using (
8.4
), we obtain that

\varphi_{1}=u^{\pm}_{1,0}-u^{\pm}_{2,0}\partial_{2}\varphi_{0}\Big|_{x_{1}=0}.

(8.5)

Denote
(H^{\pm}_{N})_{j}=\partial^{j}_{t}H^{\pm}_{N}\Big|_{t=0}.
Using (
8.3
) and (
8.5
), taking
t=0,
we obtain that

(H^{\pm}_{N})_{1}=-\Big(u^{\pm}_{2,0}\partial_{2}(H^{\pm}_{N})_{0}+\partial_{2}u^{\pm}_{2,0}(H^{\pm}_{N})_{0}\Big|_{x_{1}=0}\Big).

Therefore,
(H^{\pm}_{N})_{0}|_{x_{1}=0}=0
implies
(H^{\pm}_{N})_{1}|_{x_{1}=0}=0.

Once we have defined
{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\varphi_{1},
we can deduce
{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\varphi_{2}
and so on. Moreover, at each step, we can prove that

(H^{\pm}_{N})_{j}|_{x_{1}=0}=0,\quad j\geq 2,

provided that
{\mathbf{U}}_{j}
,
\varphi_{j}
satisfy the compatibility condition (see Definition
8.1
).

The following Lemma
8.1
is necessary for the approximate solutions; we refer to
[
14
]
and
[
20
, Lemma 4.2.1]
. Differently from
[
39
]
, we take the initial data in the standard Sobolev spaces.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{N},\mu\geq 3,

\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{0}:=\mathbf{U}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}\in H^{\mu+1.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
\varphi_{0}\in H^{\mu+1.5}({\mathbb{R}})
.
Then, we can determine
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}\in H^{\mu+1.5-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and
\varphi_{j}\in H^{\mu+1.5-j}({\mathbb{R}})
by induction and set
\mathbf{U}_{j}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{j}+\bar{\mathbf{U}},
for
j=1,\cdots,\mu
.
Besides we prove

\sum^{\mu}_{j=0}(||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}||_{H^{\mu+1.5-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}+||\varphi_{j}||_{H^{\mu+1.5-j}({\mathbb{R}})})\leq C(M_{0}),

(8.6)

where
C>0
depends only on
\mu
,
||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{0}||_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}
and
||\varphi_{0}||_{W^{1,\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}
, and

M_{0}:=||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{0}||_{{H^{\mu+1.5}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})}+||\varphi_{0}||_{H^{\mu+1.5}({\mathbb{R}})}\,.

(8.7)

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{N},\mu\geq 3
. The initial data
(\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{0},\varphi_{0})\in H^{\mu+1.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times H^{\mu+1.5}({\mathbb{R}})
are defined to be compatible up to order
\mu
if
(\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j},\varphi_{j})
satisfy (
8.4
) for
j=0

and

\sum^{j}_{l=0}([u_{1,j-l}]-[u_{2,j-l}]\partial_{2}\varphi_{l})=0,\quad[p_{j}]+\sum^{j-1}_{l=0}C_{l,j-1}[(H_{l},H_{j-l})]=0,\quad\mbox{on}\,\,\{x_{1}=0\}\,,

for
j=1,\cdots,\mu
, where
C_{l,j-1}
are suitable constants.

To use the tame estimate for the proof of convergence of the Nash–Moser iteration, we should reduce our nonlinear problem to that whose solution vanishes in the past. This is achieved by the construction of the so-called “approximate solution” that allows to “absorb” the initial data into the interior equation. The “approximate solution” is in the sense of Taylor’s series at
t=0
.

Below, we will use the notation

\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}},\Psi):=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{+},\Psi^{+})\\
\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{-},\Psi^{-})\end{array}\right].\quad

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{N},\mu\geq 3
and let
\delta>0
. Suppose the initial data
(\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{0},\varphi_{0})\in H^{\mu+1.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times H^{\mu+1.5}({\mathbb{R}})
are compatible up to order
\mu
and satisfy the assumptions (
1.6
), (
2.4
), (
2.5
), (
4.4
). Then, there exist
T>0
and
({\tilde{\mathbf{U}}}^{a},\varphi^{a})\in H^{\mu+2}(\Omega_{T})\times H^{\mu+2}(\Gamma_{T})
such that

\partial^{j}_{t}\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{a},\Psi^{a})|_{t=0}=0\text{ in }\Omega,\text{ for }j\in\{0,\cdots,\mu-1\},

(8.8)

where

\mathbf{U}^{a}:=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a}+\overline{\mathbf{U}}\,,\quad\Psi^{a\,\pm}=\chi(\pm x_{1})\varphi^{a}\,.

We call
(\mathbf{U}^{a},\varphi^{a})
the approximate solution to problem (
4.20
). Moreover the approximate solution satisfies the following estimate

||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{a}||_{H^{\mu+2}(\Omega_{T})}+||\varphi^{a}||_{H^{\mu+2}(\Gamma_{T})}<\delta\,,

(8.9)

the stability conditions (
4.4
) on
\Gamma_{T}
, the hyperbolicity condition (
4.2
) on
\Omega_{T}
.

Proof.

Let us first denote
\Phi^{a\,\pm}=\pm x_{1}+\Psi^{a\,\pm}
,

\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a}=(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a+},\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a-})^{T}
,
\tilde{p}^{a}=(\tilde{p}^{a+},\tilde{p}^{a-})^{T}
,
\tilde{u}^{a}=(\tilde{u}^{a+},\tilde{u}^{a-})^{T}
,
\tilde{H}^{a}=(\tilde{H}^{a+},\tilde{H}^{a-})^{T}
,

\tilde{S}^{a}=(\tilde{S}^{a+},\tilde{S}^{a-})^{T}
.
Consider
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a}\in H^{\mu+2}({\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}),\varphi^{a}\in H^{\mu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, such that

\partial^{j}_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a}|_{t=0}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{j}\in H^{\mu-j+2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}),\text{ for }j=0,\cdots,\mu,

\partial^{j}_{t}\varphi^{a}|_{t=0}=\varphi_{j}\in H^{\mu-j+2}({\mathbb{R}}),\quad\text{ for }j=0,\cdots,\mu,

where
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{j}
and
\varphi_{j}
are given by Lemma
8.1
. Since
(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a},\varphi^{a})
satisfies the hyperbolicity condition (
4.2
) and the stability condition (
4.4
) at
t=0
, by continuity
(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a},\varphi^{a})
satisfy (
4.4
) at
x_{1}=0
and (
4.2
) for small times. By multiplication of
(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a},\varphi^{a})
by a cut-off function in time supported on
[-T,T]
we can assume that (
4.2
), (
4.4
) hold for all times (in this regard, recall Remark
3.1
). Given any
\delta>0
, by taking
T>0
sufficiently small, we can assume that
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}^{a}
,
\varphi^{a}
are small in the sense of (
8.9
).
∎

Remark 8.1
.

Let us remark that we do not require any constraint (that is interior equations or boundary condition) to be satisfied by the approximate solution constructed above. This allows us to the use of cut-off argument making the hyperbolicity condition (
4.2
) and the stability condition (
4.4
) to be satisfied globally in time, without any trouble.

Remark 8.2
.

In the sequel, in the proof of the main Theorem
3.1
, estimate (
8.9
) will be used with
\mu=m+10
, being
m
an integer as in the statement of that theorem.

We assume that

||\varphi_{0}||_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}<\frac{1}{2},

then we fix
T>0
sufficiently small so that
||\varphi^{a}||_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}})}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Hence, we get

\partial_{1}\Phi^{a+}\geq\frac{1}{2},\quad\partial_{1}\Phi^{a-}\leq-\frac{1}{2}

(recall that
||\chi^{\prime}||_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq 1/2
, see Section
2
).

The approximate solution
({\mathbf{U}}^{a},\varphi^{a})
enables us to reformulate the original problem (
2.1
) as a nonlinear problem with zero initial data. Set

\mathcal{F}^{a}:=\begin{cases}-\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{a},\Psi^{a}),\;&t>0,\\
~~~~0,\;&t<0.\\
\end{cases}

(8.10)

From
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{a}\in H^{\mu+2}(\Omega_{T})
and
\varphi^{a}\in H^{\mu+2}(\Gamma_{T}),
we have
\mathcal{F}^{a}\in H^{\mu+1}(\Omega_{T})
.

Given the approximate solution
(\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{a},\varphi^{a})
of Lemma
8.2
and
\mathcal{F}^{a}
defined in (
8.10
), we see that
({\mathbf{U}},\varphi)=({\mathbf{U}}^{a},\varphi^{a})+({\mathbf{V}},\psi)
is a solution of the original problem (
2.1
) if
{\mathbf{V}}=({\mathbf{V}}^{+},{\mathbf{V}}^{-})^{T},\Psi=(\Psi^{+},\Psi^{-})^{T},

\Psi|_{x_{1}=0}:=\psi
satisfy the following problem:

\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}},\Psi):=\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi)-\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{a},\Psi^{a})=\mathcal{F}^{a},&\text{in }\Omega_{T},\\
\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi):=\mathbb{B}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}|_{x_{1}=0}+{\mathbf{V}}|_{x_{1}=0},\varphi^{a}+\psi)=0,&\text{on }\Gamma_{T},\\
({\mathbf{V}},\psi)=0,&\text{for }t<0.\end{cases}

(8.11)

The original nonlinear problem on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
is thus reformulated as a problem on
\Omega_{T}
whose solutions vanish in the past.

9.
Nash-Moser Iteration

In this section, we recall the Nash-Moser iteration for reader’s convenience. First, we introduce the smoothing operators
S_{\theta}
and describe the iterative scheme for problem (
8.11
). For more details refer to
[
5
,
14
,
39
]
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{N},
with
\mu\geq 4
.
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T}):=\{u\in H^{s}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T}):u=0\text{ for }t<0\}.
Define a family of smoothing operators
\{S_{\theta}\}_{{\theta\geq 1}}
on the anisotropic Sobolev space from
\mathcal{F}^{3}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})
to
\bigcap_{s\geq 3}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})
, such that

||S_{\theta}u||_{k,\ast,T}\leq C\theta^{(k-j)_{+}}||u||_{j,\ast,T},\text{ for all }k,j\in\{1,\cdots,\mu\},

(9.1)

||S_{\theta}u-u||_{k,\ast,T}\leq C\theta^{k-j}||u||_{j,\ast,T},\text{ for all }1\leq k\leq j\leq\mu,

(9.2)

||\frac{d}{d\theta}S_{\theta}u||_{k,\ast,T}\leq C\theta^{k-j-1}||u||_{j,\ast,T},\text{ for all }k,j\in\{1,\cdots,\mu\},

(9.3)

where
C
is positive constant and
k,j\in\mathbb{N},(k-j)_{+}:=\max\{0,k-j\}.
In particular, if
u=v
on
\Gamma_{T},
then
S_{\theta}u=S_{\theta}v
on
\Gamma_{T}
. The definition of
\mathcal{F}^{s}(\Gamma_{T})
is entirely similar.

Now, we begin to formulate the Nash-Moser iteration scheme.

The iteration scheme starts from
({\mathbf{V}}_{0},\Psi_{0},\psi_{0})=(0,0,0),
and
({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi_{i},\psi_{i})
is given such that

({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi_{i},\psi_{i})|_{t<0}=0,\quad\Psi^{+}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0}=\Psi^{-}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0}=\psi_{i}.

(9.4)

Let us consider

{\mathbf{V}}_{i+1}={\mathbf{V}}_{i}+\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\;\Psi_{i+1}=\Psi_{i}+\delta\Psi_{i},\;\psi_{i+1}=\psi_{i}+\delta\psi_{i},

(9.5)

where the differences
(\delta{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i},\delta\psi_{i})
will be determined below.
First, we can obtain
(\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i},\delta\psi_{i})
by solving the effective linear problem:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{e}^{\prime}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i}=f_{i}&\text{ in }\Omega_{T},\\
\displaystyle\mathbb{B}_{e}^{\prime}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})(\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i},\delta\psi_{i})=g_{i}&\text{ on }\Gamma_{T},\\
\displaystyle(\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i},\delta\psi_{i})=0&\text{ for }t<0,\end{cases}

(9.6)

where operators
\mathbb{L}_{e}^{\prime},\mathbb{B}_{e}^{\prime}
are defined in (
4.15
) and (
4.16
),

\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i}:=\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i}-\frac{\partial_{1}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}})}{\partial_{1}(\Phi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})}\delta\Psi_{i}

(9.7)

is the Alinhac “good unknown” and
({\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
is a smooth modified state such that
({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
satisfies (
4.2
)–(
4.8
) and (
4.10
). The source terms
(f_{i},g_{i})
will be defined through the accumulated errors at step
i
.

S_{\theta_{i}}
is the smoothing operator with
{\theta_{i}}
defined by

\theta_{0}\geq 1,\;\theta_{i}=\sqrt{\theta^{2}_{0}+i}\,.

(9.8)

The errors at step
i
can be defined from the following decompositions:

\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}}_{i+1},\Psi_{i+1})-\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi_{i})\\
&=\mathbb{L}^{\prime}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\delta\Psi_{i})+e^{\prime}_{i}\\
&=\mathbb{L}^{\prime}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+S_{\theta_{i}}{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi^{a}+S_{\theta_{i}}\Psi_{i})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\delta\Psi_{i})+e^{\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime}_{i}\\
&=\mathbb{L}^{\prime}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\delta\Psi_{i})+e^{\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}\\
&=\mathbb{L}^{\prime}_{e}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i}+e^{\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}+D_{i+\frac{1}{2}}\delta\Psi_{i}\\
\end{split}

(9.9)

and

\begin{split}&\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i+1}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{i+1})-\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{i})\\
&=\mathbb{B}^{\prime}(({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i})|_{x_{1}=0},\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{i})+\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{i}\\
&=\mathbb{B}^{\prime}(({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+S_{\theta_{i}}{\mathbf{V}}_{i})|_{x_{1}=0},\varphi^{a}+S_{\theta_{i}}\Psi_{i}|_{x_{1}=0})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{i})+\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{i}+\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\\
&=\mathbb{B}^{\prime}_{e}(({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}})|_{x_{1}=0},\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})(\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{i})+\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{i}+\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{i}+\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i},\\
\end{split}

(9.10)

where we write

D_{i+\frac{1}{2}}:=\frac{1}{\partial_{1}(\Phi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})}\partial_{1}\mathbb{L}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}}),

(9.11)

and have used (
4.14
) to get the last identity in (
9.9
).
Denote

e_{i}:=e^{\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime}_{i}+e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}+D_{i+\frac{1}{2}}\delta\Psi_{i},\quad\tilde{e}_{i}:=\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{i}+\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{i}+\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i}.

(9.12)

We assume
f_{0}:=S_{\theta_{0}}\mathcal{F}^{a},(E_{0},\tilde{E}_{0},g_{0}):=(0,0,0)
and
(f_{k},g_{k},e_{k},\tilde{e}_{k})
are already given and vanish in the past for
k\in\{0,\cdots,i-1\}.

We can calculate the accumulated errors at step
i,i\geq 1
, by

E_{i}:=\sum^{i-1}_{k=0}e_{k},\quad\tilde{E}_{i}:=\sum^{i-1}_{k=0}\tilde{e}_{k}.

(9.13)

Then, we obtain
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
for
i\geq 1
from the equations:

\sum^{i}_{k=0}f_{k}+S_{\theta_{i}}E_{i}=S_{\theta_{i}}\mathcal{F}^{a},\quad\sum^{i}_{k=0}g_{k}+S_{\theta_{i}}\tilde{E}_{i}=0.

(9.14)

Then, given suitable
({\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
, we can obtain
(\delta\dot{{\mathbf{V}}}_{i},\delta\psi_{i})
as the solutions of the linear problem (
9.6
),
\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{i}
from (
9.7
),
({\mathbf{V}}_{i+1},\Psi_{i+1},\psi_{i+1})
from (
9.5
). Since
S_{\theta_{i}}\rightarrow I
as
i\rightarrow\infty,
we can formally obtain the solution to problem (
8.11
) from
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{a},\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{i})\rightarrow 0,
as error terms
(e_{i},\tilde{e}_{i})\rightarrow 0.

10.
Proof of the Main Result

Now, we prove the local existence of solutions to (
8.11
) by a modified iteration scheme of Nash-Moser type.
From the sequence
\{\theta_{i}\}
defined in (
9.8
), we set
\Delta_{i}:=\theta_{i+1}-\theta_{i}.
Then, the sequence
\{\Delta_{i}\}
is decreasing and tends to
0
as
i
goes to infinity. Moreover, we have

\frac{1}{3\theta_{i}}\leq\Delta_{i}=\sqrt{\theta^{2}_{i}+1}-\theta_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2\theta_{i}},\;\forall\,i\in\mathbb{N}.

10.1.
Inductive analysis

Given a small fixed
\delta>0
, and an integer
\tilde{\alpha}
that will be chosen later on, we assume that the following estimate holds:

||\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{a}||_{\tilde{\alpha}+6,\ast,T}+||\varphi^{a}||_{H^{\tilde{\alpha}+6}(\Gamma_{T})}+||\mathcal{F}^{a}||_{\tilde{\alpha}+4,\ast,T}\leq\delta.

(10.1)

We may assume that (
10.1
) holds, by taking
T>0
sufficiently small.

Given the integer
\alpha
, our inductive assumptions read

\displaystyle(H_{i-1})\left\{\begin{split}&\displaystyle(a)\quad||(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k})||_{s,\ast,T}+||\delta\psi_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq\delta\theta^{s-\alpha-1}_{k}\Delta_{k},\\
&\qquad\forall k\in\{0,\cdots,i-1\},\quad\forall s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}\}.\\
&\displaystyle(b)\quad||\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})-\mathcal{F}^{a}||_{s,\ast,T}\leq 2\delta\theta^{s-\alpha-1}_{k},\\
&\qquad\forall k\in\{0,\cdots,i-1\},\quad\forall s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}-2\}.\\
&\displaystyle(c)\quad||\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{k})||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq\delta\theta^{s-\alpha-1}_{k},\\
&\qquad\forall k\in\{0,\cdots,i-1\},\quad\forall s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}-2\}.\\
\end{split}\right.

(10.2)

Our goal is to show that
(H_{0})
holds and
(H_{i-1})
implies
(H_{i})
, for a suitable choice of the parameters
\alpha
,
\tilde{\alpha}
, for
\delta>0
and
T>0
sufficiently small, for
\theta_{0}\geq 1
sufficiently large. Then, we conclude that
(H_{i})
holds for all
i\in\mathbb{N}.

Lemma 10.1
.

If
T>0
is sufficiently small, then
(H_{0})
holds.

Proof.

The proof follows as in
[
39
, Lemma 17]
.
∎

Now we prove that
(H_{i-1})
implies
(H_{i})
. The hypothesis
(H_{i-1})
yields the following lemma.

Lemma 10.2
.

[
39
, Lemma 7]
,
[
14
, Lemma 7]

If
\theta_{0}
is large enough, then, for each
k\in\{0,\cdots,i\}
, and each integer
s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}\},

||({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})||_{s,\ast,T}+||\psi_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\delta\theta^{(s-\alpha)_{+}}_{k},&\text{if }s\neq\alpha,\\
\delta\log\theta_{k},&\text{if }s=\alpha,\\
\end{cases}

(10.3)

||(I-S_{\theta_{k}})({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})||_{s,\ast,T}+||(I-S_{\theta_{k}})\psi_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim\delta\theta^{s-\alpha}_{k}.

(10.4)

Furthermore, for each
k\in\{0,\cdots,i\}
, and each integer
s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}+8\},

||(S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k},S_{\theta_{k}}\Psi_{k})||_{s,\ast,T}+||S_{\theta_{k}}\psi_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim\begin{cases}\delta\theta^{(s-\alpha)_{+}}_{k},&\text{if }s\neq\alpha,\\
\delta\log\theta_{k},&\text{if }s=\alpha.\\
\end{cases}

(10.5)

10.2.
Estimate of the error terms

To derive
(H_{i})
from
(H_{i-1})
, we need to estimate the quadratic error terms
e^{\prime}_{k}
and
\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{k},
the first substitution error terms
e^{\prime\prime}_{k}
and
\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{k},
the second substitution error terms
e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k}
and
\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{k}
and the last error term
D_{k+\frac{1}{2}}\delta\Psi_{k}
(
cfr.
(
9.9
)-(
9.11
)).

First, we denote the quadratic error terms by

e^{\prime}_{k}:=\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}}_{k+1},\Psi_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k}),

(10.6)

\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{k}:=\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{k+1}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{k+1})-\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{k})-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}({\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{k})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{k}).

(10.7)

Then, we get

e^{\prime}_{k}=\int^{1}_{0}\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{k}+\tau\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{k}+\tau\delta\Psi_{k})((\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k}),(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k}))(1-\tau)d\tau,

\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{k}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}((\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\psi_{k}),(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\psi_{k})),

where
\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}
denote the second order derivatives of the operators
\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{B}.

To be more precise, we define

\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\Psi})(({\mathbf{V}},\Psi),(\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}},\tilde{\Psi})):=\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\mathbb{L}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}+\varepsilon\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}},\hat{\Psi}+\varepsilon\tilde{\Psi})({\mathbf{V}},\Psi)\Big|_{\varepsilon=0},

\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}(({\mathbf{V}},\psi),(\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}},\tilde{\psi})):=\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\mathbb{B}^{\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}+\varepsilon\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}},\hat{\varphi}+\varepsilon\tilde{\psi})({\mathbf{V}},\psi)\Big|_{\varepsilon=0},

where
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
are defined in (
4.11
) and (
4.12
). Simple calculations yield that

\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}(({\mathbf{V}},\psi),(\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}},\tilde{\psi})):=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\tilde{u}^{+}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi+\partial_{2}\tilde{\psi}u^{+}_{2}\\
\tilde{u}^{-}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi+\partial_{2}\tilde{\psi}u^{-}_{2}\\
\mathbf{H}^{+}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{H}}^{+}-\mathbf{H}^{-}\cdot\tilde{\mathbf{H}}^{-}\end{array}\right]\,.

(10.8)

To estimate the error terms, we need to estimate the operators
\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}.
Applying the Moser-type calculus inequalities in Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
and the explicit forms of
\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}
, we can obtain the necessary estimates. Omitting the detailed calculation, we have the following Lemma
10.3
:

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
T>0,
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
with
s\geq 6.
Assume that
(\breve{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\Psi})\in H^{s+2}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})
satisfies

||(\hat{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\Psi})||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}\leq\tilde{K},

(recall that
\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}=\bar{{\mathbf{U}}}+\breve{{\mathbf{U}}}
) for some constant
\tilde{K}>0.
Then, there exists a positive
C
depending on
\tilde{K},
but not on
T,
such that if
({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi_{i})\in H^{s+2}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})
and
(W_{i},\psi_{i})\in H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})\times H^{s+1}(\Gamma_{T}),
for
i=1,2,

then

\begin{split}&||\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}(\hat{\mathbf{U}},\hat{\Psi})(({\mathbf{V}}_{1},\Psi_{1}),({\mathbf{V}}_{2},\Psi_{2}))||_{s,\ast,T}\\
&\quad\leq C||(\breve{{\mathbf{U}}},\hat{\Psi})||_{s+2,\ast,T}||({\mathbf{V}}_{1},\Psi_{1})||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}||({\mathbf{V}}_{2},\Psi_{2})||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}\\
&\quad\quad+C\sum_{i\neq j}||({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\Psi_{i})||_{s+2,\ast,T}||({\mathbf{V}}_{j},\Psi_{j})||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&||\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}((W_{1},\psi_{1}),(W_{2},\psi_{2}))||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\quad\leq C\sum_{i\neq j}\Big(||W_{i}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}||\psi_{j}||_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}+||W_{i}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||\psi_{j}||_{H^{s+1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\quad\quad+||W_{i}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}||W_{j}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}\Big).\end{split}

10.2.1.
Estimate of the quadratic errors

We now apply Lemma
10.3
to prove the following estimate for the quadratic error terms.

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
\alpha\geq 7.
There exist
\delta>0
sufficiently small and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
sufficiently large such that, for all
k\in\{0,\cdots,i-1\},
and all integers
s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}-2\},
we have

||e^{\prime}_{k}||_{s,\ast,T}\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{1}(s)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k},

(10.9)

||\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{1}(s)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k},

(10.10)

where
L_{1}(s):=\max\{(s+2-\alpha)_{+}+10-2\alpha;s+6-2\alpha\}.

Proof.

Using (
10.1
), the hypothesis
(H_{i-1})
and the estimate (
10.3
), we use the Sobolev inequalities (
3.10
) to get

||(\mathbf{U}^{a},{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi^{a},\Psi_{k},\delta\Psi_{k})||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim 1.

Then, we apply Lemma
10.3
and use Sobolev inequalities (
3.10
), the assumption (
10.1
) and the hypothesis
(H_{i-1})
to give

\begin{split}||e^{\prime}_{k}||_{s,\ast,T}&\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{10-2\alpha}_{k}\Delta^{2}_{k}(1+||({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})||_{s+2,\ast,T}+\delta\theta^{s+1-\alpha}_{k}\Delta_{k})\\
&\quad\quad+||(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k})||_{s+2,\ast,T}||(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k})||_{6,\ast,T}\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{10-2\alpha}_{k}\Delta^{2}_{k}(1+||({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})||_{s+2,\ast,T})+\delta^{2}\theta^{s+6-2\alpha}_{k}\Delta^{2}_{k},\end{split}

for all
s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}-2\}.

If
s+2\neq\alpha,
then it follows from (
10.3
) and
2\theta_{k}\Delta_{k}\leq 1,
that

\begin{split}||e^{\prime}_{k}||_{s,\ast,T}&\lesssim\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}_{k}(\theta^{(s+2-\alpha)_{+}+10-2\alpha}_{k}+\theta^{s+6-2\alpha}_{k})\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{1}(s)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k}.\end{split}

If
s+2=\alpha,
then it follows from (
10.3
) and
\alpha\geq 7,
that

\begin{split}||e^{\prime}_{k}||_{s,\ast,T}&\lesssim\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}_{k}(\theta^{11-2\alpha}_{k}+\theta^{4-\alpha}_{k})\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{1}(\alpha-2)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k}.\end{split}

Therefore, we obtain (
10.9
).
Now, we prove (
10.10
). Using Lemma
10.3
and trace Theorem
A.1
, we obtain

\begin{split}||\tilde{e}^{\prime}_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}&\lesssim||\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}||\delta\psi_{k}||_{W^{1,\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}+||\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||\delta\psi_{k}||_{H^{s+1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\quad+||\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}||\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{1}(s)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k}.\end{split}

This completes the proof of Lemma
10.4
.
∎

10.2.2.
Estimate of the first substitution errors

We can estimate the first substitution errors
e^{\prime\prime}_{k},\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{k}
of the iteration scheme, defined in (
9.9
) and (
9.10
). We rewrite

e^{\prime\prime}_{k}:=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}({\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi_{k})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k})-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k},S_{\theta_{k}}\Psi_{k})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k}),

(10.11)

\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{k}:=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}({\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{k})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{k})-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},S_{\theta_{k}}\psi_{k})(\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{k}).

(10.12)

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
\alpha\geq 7.
There exist
\delta>0
sufficiently small and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
sufficiently large, such that for all
k\in\{0,\cdots,i-1\}
and for all integer
s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}-2\},
we have

||e^{\prime\prime}_{k}||_{s,\ast,T}\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{2}(s)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k},

(10.13)

||\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{k}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{L_{2}(s)-1}_{k}\Delta_{k},

(10.14)

where
L_{2}(s):=\max\{(s+2-\alpha)_{+}+12-2\alpha;s+8-2\alpha\}.

Proof.

In view of (
10.11
) and (
10.12
) we have

\begin{split}e^{\prime\prime}_{k}&=\int^{1}_{0}\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{U}^{a}+S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k}+\tau(I-S_{\theta_{k}}){\mathbf{V}}_{k},\Psi^{a}+S_{\theta_{k}}\Psi_{k}+\tau(I-S_{\theta_{k}})\Psi_{k})\\
&\quad\quad((\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k},\delta\Psi_{k}),((I-S_{\theta_{k}}){\mathbf{V}}_{k},(I-S_{\theta_{k}})\Psi_{k}))d\tau,\\
\tilde{e}^{\prime\prime}_{k}&=\mathbb{B}^{\prime\prime}((\delta{\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},\delta\psi_{k}),((I-S_{\theta_{k}}){\mathbf{V}}_{k}|_{x_{1}=0},(I-S_{\theta_{k}})\psi_{k})).\end{split}

Using (
10.4
) and (
10.5
), we have

||(S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k},{\mathbf{V}}_{k},S_{\theta_{k}}\Psi_{k},\Psi_{k})||_{W^{2,\infty}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})}\lesssim||(S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k},{\mathbf{V}}_{k},S_{\theta_{k}}\Psi_{k},\Psi_{k})||_{6,\ast,T}\lesssim 1.

Next, we apply Lemma
10.3
, use Sobolev inequalities (
3.10
), (
10.1
), the Hypothesis
(H_{i-1})
and (
10.4
) to get that

||e^{\prime\prime}_{k}||_{s,\ast,T}\lesssim\delta^{2}\theta^{11-2\alpha}_{k}\Delta_{k}(1+||(S_{\theta_{k}}{\mathbf{V}}_{k},S_{\theta_{k}}\Psi_{k})||_{s+2,\ast,T})+\delta^{2}\theta^{s+7-2\alpha}_{k}\Delta_{k},

for all
s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}-2\}.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
10.4
, we can discuss
s+2\neq\alpha
and
s+2=\alpha
separately. Hence, using (
10.5
), we can obtain (
10.13
) and (
10.14
). The proof of Lemma
10.5
is completed.
∎

10.2.3.
Estimate of the modified state

We need to construct a smooth modified state
({\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
such that
({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
satisfies the nonlinear constraints (
4.2
)–(
4.8
), (
4.10
) and (
6.1
). In this regard, we remark it is crucial that the sum of the approximate solution
({\mathbf{U}}^{a},\varphi^{a})
and the modified state
({\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
, instead of the latter two separately, satisfies the aforementioned nonlinear constraints; indeed it is just this sum which plays the role of basic state around which we need to linearize problem (
2.1
) in the iteration scheme leading to its solution, see problem (
9.6
).
In the construction of some components of the modified state we follow the similar approach of
[
1
,
14
,
34
]
, while for the magnetic field we are inspired by
[
34
,
39
]
.

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
\alpha\geq 10
. There exist some functions
{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}}
vanishing in the past, such that

({\mathbf{U}}^{a}+{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\Psi^{a}+\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}},\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}})
satisfy the constraints (
4.2
)– (
4.8
), (
4.10
) and (
6.1
); moreover,

\Psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}}^{\pm}=S_{\theta_{i}}\Psi^{\pm}_{i}\,,\quad\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}}=(S_{\theta_{i}}\Psi^{\pm}_{i})|_{x_{1}=0,},

(10.15)

p^{\pm}_{i+\frac{1}{2}}=S_{\theta_{i}}p^{\pm}_{i}\,,\quad u^{\pm}_{2,i+\frac{1}{2}}=S_{\theta_{i}}u^{\pm}_{2,i}\,,\quad S^{\pm}_{i+\frac{1}{2}}=S_{\theta_{i}}S^{\pm}_{i},

(10.16)

||{\mathbf{V}}_{i+\frac{1}{2}}-S_{\theta_{i}}{\mathbf{V}}_{i}||_{s,\ast,T}\lesssim\delta\theta^{s+4-\alpha}_{i},\text{ for }s\in\{6,\cdots,\tilde{\alpha}+4\},

(10.17)

for sufficiently small
\delta>0
and
T>0,
and sufficiently large
\theta_{0}\geq 1.

Proof.

To shortcut notation, in the proof the
\pm
indices are omitted. Let us define
\Psi_{i+1/2}
,
\psi_{i+1/2}
,
p_{i+1/2}
,
S_{i+1/2}
and the tangential component
u_{2,i+1/2}
of the velocity as in (
10.15
), (
10.16
); this is similar as in
[
1
]
,
[
14
, Proposition 7]
,
[
34
, Proposition 28]
. It is easily checked that all these functions vanish in the past.

Construction of the modified normal velocity.

In order to construct the normal component
u_{1,i+1/2}
of the velocity, we follow the idea of
[
1
,
14
]
. We first introduce the following function
\mathcal{G}
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}:=&\partial_{t}(\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i+1/2})-(u^{a}_{1}+S_{\theta_{i}}u_{1,i})|_{x_{1}=0}+u^{a}_{2}|_{x_{1}=0}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}\\
&+(u^{a}_{2}+u_{2,i+1/2})|_{x_{1}=0}\partial_{2}\psi_{i+1/2}+u_{2,i+1/2}|_{x_{1}=0}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}\,.\end{split}

The normal component of the velocity
u_{1,i+1/2}
is defined by

u_{1,i+1/2}:=S_{\theta_{i}}u_{1,i}+\mathcal{R}_{T}\mathcal{G}\,,

(10.18)

where
\mathcal{R}_{T}
is the lifting operator
H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})\rightarrow H^{s}_{\ast}(\Omega_{T})
,
s>1
, see
[
27
]
. It is easily checked that
u_{1,i+1/2}
vanishes in the past.

Let us note that, by construction,
u_{1,i+1/2}
satisfies the following equation on the boundary

\partial_{t}(\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i+1/2})-(u_{1}^{a}+u_{1,i+1/2})+(u_{2}^{a}+u_{2,i+1/2})\partial_{2}(\varphi^{a}+\psi_{i+1/2})=0\,,\quad\mbox{on}\,\,\,\{x_{1}=0\}\,.

(10.19)

We prove the estimate (
10.17
) for the part regarding
{\mathbf{u}}_{i+1/2}
. We have

||\mathbf{u}_{i+1/2}-S_{\theta_{i}}{\mathbf{u}}_{i}||_{s,\ast,T}=||\mathcal{R}_{T}\mathcal{G}||_{s,\ast,T}\lesssim||\mathcal{G}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\,.

Now we rewrite
\mathcal{G}
in a more convenient form, by using the error
\varepsilon^{i}
defined by

\begin{split}\varepsilon^{i}:=&\partial_{t}\psi_{i}-u_{1,i}|_{x_{1}=0}+(\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u_{1}^{a}|_{x_{1}=0}+u_{2}^{a}|_{x_{1}=0}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})+(u^{a}_{2}+u_{2,i})|_{x_{1}=0}\partial_{2}\psi_{i}\\
&+u_{2,i}|_{x_{1}=0}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}=\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i}|_{x_{1}=0},\psi_{i})_{1}\,.\end{split}

(10.20)

In view of (
10.16
), by means of
\varepsilon^{i}
we may rewrite
\mathcal{G}
as:

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}&=S_{\theta_{i}}\varepsilon^{i}-[S_{\theta_{i}},\partial_{t}]\psi_{i}+(I-S_{\theta_{i}})(\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u^{a}_{1}+u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})-S_{\theta_{i}}((u^{a}_{2}+u_{2,i})\partial_{2}\psi_{i})\\
&\quad-S_{\theta_{i}}(u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})+(u^{a}_{2}+S_{\theta_{i}}u_{2,i})\partial_{2}\psi_{i+1/2}+S_{\theta_{i}}u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}\\
&=S_{\theta_{i}}\varepsilon^{i}-[S_{\theta_{i}},\partial_{t}]\psi_{i}+(I-S_{\theta_{i}})(\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u^{a}_{1}+u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}))\\
&\quad-\left(S_{\theta_{i}}(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})-u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}\right)-\left(S_{\theta_{i}}u_{2,i}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})\right)\\
&\quad-\left(S_{\theta_{i}}u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})\right).\end{split}

(10.21)

To estimate the first term
S_{\theta_{i}}\varepsilon^{i}
on the right-hand side, we use the decomposition:

\begin{split}\varepsilon^{i}&=\left(\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i},\psi_{i})_{1}-\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i-1},\psi_{i-1})_{1}\right)+\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i-1},\psi_{i-1})_{1}\\
&=\partial_{t}\delta\psi_{i-1}-\delta u_{1,i-1}+(u_{2}^{a}+u_{2,i-1})\partial_{2}\delta\psi_{i-1}+\delta u_{2,i-1}\partial_{2}(\varphi^{a}+\delta\psi_{i-1}+\psi_{i-1})\\
&\quad+\mathcal{B}({\mathbf{V}}_{i-1},\psi_{i-1})_{1}\,.\end{split}

Then we exploit point
(c)
of (
H_{i-1}
) and the properties of smoothing operators, to get

||S_{\theta_{i}}\varepsilon^{i}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim||\varepsilon^{i}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha-1}\,.

(10.22)

In order to make an estimate of the commutator term
[S_{\theta_{i}},\partial_{t}]\psi_{i}
, we use different arguments for large and small orders
s
.

For all
s\in\{6,\dots,\alpha\}
we write the commutator as

[\partial_{t},S_{\theta_{i}}]\psi_{i}=\partial_{t}(S_{\theta_{i}}-I)\psi_{i}+(I-S_{\theta_{i}})\partial_{t}\psi_{i}\,,

then we use estimates (
9.2
), (
10.3
), (
10.4
) to get

||[\partial_{t},S_{\theta_{i}}]\psi_{i}||_{s,\ast,T}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad s\in\{6,\dots,\alpha\}\,.

(10.23)

In order to get the similar estimate as (
10.23
) for
s\in\{\alpha+1,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}
, we directly estimate the two terms of the commutator
[\partial_{t},S_{\theta_{i}}]\psi_{i}=\partial_{t}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}\partial_{t}\psi_{i}
, using (
9.1
) and (
10.5
).

To estimate the third term in the right-hand side of (
10.21
), we proceed as above by applying different arguments to small and large orders
s
.

For integers
s\in\{6,\dots,\alpha\}
, we apply (
9.2
) to get

\begin{split}||(I-S_{\theta_{i}})&(\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u^{a}_{1}+u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}||\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u^{a}_{1}+u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}||_{H^{\alpha-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}(||\varphi^{a}||_{H^{\alpha-1}(\Gamma_{T})}+||u^{a}||_{\alpha,\ast,T})\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\end{split}

in view of (
10.1
).

For integers
s\in\{\alpha+1,\dots.\tilde{\alpha}+6\}
, we use (
9.1
) and (
10.1
) to estimate directly

\begin{split}||(I-S_{\theta_{i}})&(\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u^{a}_{1}+u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq||E^{a}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}+||S_{\theta_{i}}E^{a}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\leq||E^{a}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}+C\theta_{i}^{(s-1-(\alpha-1))_{+}}||E^{a}||_{H^{\alpha-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}||E^{a}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}(||\varphi^{a}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}+||u^{a}||_{s,\ast,T})\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\end{split}

where
E^{a}:=\partial_{t}\varphi^{a}-u^{a}_{1}+u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}
has been set.

Let us now estimate the fourth term
-\left(S_{\theta_{i}}(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})-u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}\right)
; once again we need to argue separately on different values of
s
.

For small integers
s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}\}
we rewrite the above in the form

u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})=u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}(S_{\theta_{i}}-I)\psi_{i}+(I-S_{\theta_{i}})(u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})\,,

(10.24)

which takes advantage of the appearing of the difference
I-S_{\theta_{i}}
; thus estimate (
10.4
) and Moser-type calculus inequalities of Lemma
3.1
yield

\begin{split}||u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}(S_{\theta_{i}}-I)&\psi_{i}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim||u^{a}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||(I-S_{\theta_{i}})\psi_{i}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&+||u^{a}||_{s,\ast,T}||(I-S_{\theta_{i}})\psi_{i}||_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}\}\,.\end{split}

(10.25)

As for the second term in the right-hand side of (
10.24
), a further splitting of the range of
s
covered by estimate (
10.25
) is required. For integers
s
such that
6\leq s\leq\alpha+1
, we apply (
9.2
), Moser-type calculus inequalities of Lemma
3.1
and (
10.3
) to obtain

\begin{split}||(I-S_{\theta_{i}})&(u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-1-(\alpha+1)}||u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\psi_{i}||_{H^{\alpha+1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha-2}\left\{||u^{a}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||\psi_{i}||_{H^{\alpha+1}(\Gamma_{T})}+||u^{a}||_{\alpha+2,\ast,T}||\psi_{i}||_{H^{3}(\Gamma_{T})}\right\}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha-2}(\delta\theta_{i}+\delta)\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha-1}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,6\leq s\leq\alpha+1\,.\end{split}

(10.26)

On the other hand for integers
s
such that
\alpha+1<s\leq\tilde{\alpha}
, we use (
9.1
), Moser-type calculus inequalities and (
10.3
) to get

\begin{split}||(I-S_{\theta_{i}})&(u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C||u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}\psi_{i}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\leq C\left\{||u^{a}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||\psi_{i}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}+||u^{a}||_{s,\ast,T}||\psi_{i}||_{H^{3}(\Gamma_{T})}\right\}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{(s-\alpha)_{+}}+C\delta\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,\alpha+1<s\leq\tilde{\alpha}\,.\end{split}

(10.27)

Gathering (
10.25
)–(
10.27
) we end up with

||u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}\}\,.

(10.28)

For higher integers
s\in\{\tilde{\alpha}+1,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}
, we estimate separately the two terms of the difference
u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})
; using again estimates (
9.1
), (
10.3
), (
10.5
) and Moser-type calculus inequalities, we obtain

\begin{split}||u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\lesssim||u^{a}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}||_{H^{s}(\Gamma_{T})}+||u^{a}_{2}||_{s,\ast,T}||S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}||_{H^{3}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,;\end{split}

\begin{split}||S_{\theta_{i}}&(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-1-\alpha}||u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i}||_{H^{\alpha}(\Gamma_{T})}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-1-\alpha}\left\{||u^{a}_{2}||_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{T})}||\psi_{i}||_{H^{\alpha+1}(\Gamma_{T})}+||u^{a}_{2}||_{\alpha+1,\ast,T}||\psi_{i}||_{H^{3}(\Gamma_{T})}\right\}\\
&\leq C\theta_{i}^{s-1-\alpha}(\delta\theta_{i}+C\delta)\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,\tilde{\alpha}+1\leq s\leq\tilde{\alpha}+6\,.\end{split}

Adding the last two inequalities we end up with

||u_{2}^{a}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u^{a}_{2}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,s\in\{\tilde{\alpha}+1,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}\,.

(10.29)

Gathering estimates (
10.28
) and (
10.29
) provide the estimate for all integers
s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}
.

For the last two terms in the right-hand side of (
10.21
) we use the same arguments as above, where we still separate small and large orders
s
; in the case of small
s
we manage to rewrite the expression in order to make advantage from the boundedness properties (
9.2
), (
10.4
) of
I-S_{\theta_{i}}
; for large
s
we estimate separately each term of the difference using Moser-type calculus inequalities and (
9.1
), (
10.3
), (
10.5
). Doing so, we derive:

||S_{\theta_{i}}u_{2,i}\partial_{2}S_{\theta_{i}}\psi_{i}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\psi_{i})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha+1}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}\,;

||S_{\theta_{i}}u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a}-S_{\theta_{i}}(u_{2,i}\partial_{2}\varphi^{a})||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}\,.

Gathering all the previously found estimates we end up with

||\mathbf{u}_{i+1/2}-S_{\theta_{i}}{\mathbf{u}}_{i}||_{s,\ast,T}\lesssim||\mathcal{G}||_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma_{T})}\leq C\delta\theta_{i}^{s-\alpha+1}\,,\quad\mbox{for}\,\,\,s\in\{6,\dots,\tilde{\alpha}+6\}\,.

(10.30)

In the above estimate it is fundamental that
s\leq\tilde{\alpha}+6
in order to prove the following (
10.40
). Let us recall that the above estimate holds under the smallness assumption (
10.1
).

Construction of the modified magnetic field.
